# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Overall / Domain Description
This is a console pipe-connection game. The game board is a grid surrounded by outer walls. Inside the board there is one source, and on the border there is one sink. The player places pipes from a pipe queue. After an initial delay, water begins to flow through connected pipes.
The board uses zero-based internal array indices. However, index 0 and the last row/column are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts from internal index 1 and ends at rows - 2 / cols - 2.
Player-facing coordinates intentionally align with these playable internal indices:
row 1 refers to internal row 1, and column 'A' refers to internal column 1. No row-minus-one or column-minus-one conversion is applied, except that column letters are mapped so that 'A' becomes 1.
Directions include up, down, left, and right. Each direction has a corresponding opposite direction and a unit movement offset(class PA19Helper will help class Direction to provide these functions). Pipe shapes define which directions they connect to. Termination type distinguishes source and sink.

Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction
A coordinate stores the internal row and column used directly to access the cell grid. A cell stored at a given grid position should carry the same coordinate value.
Because the outer border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally begin at index 1. This allows user input to align directly with the playable internal grid.
Directions support movement calculations through their unit offsets and can be compared against their opposite direction when checking pipe connectivity.

Game
The game supports placing the current pipe, skipping the current pipe, undoing the latest placement, advancing the water state, checking whether the player has won, checking whether the player has lost, and tracking the number of player steps.
When the player places a pipe, the row value is used directly. The column letter is converted so that 'A' maps to column 1, 'B' maps to column 2, and so on. For example, row 1 and column 'A' target the internal cell at [1][1].
Skipping removes the current pipe from the front of the queue and counts as one step.
Undo restores the latest placed pipe to the front of the queue, clears that pipe from the board, and counts as one step. If there is no previous placement to undo, the operation fails without changing the board.

Map
The map stores cells in a two-dimensional grid with indices from [0..rows-1] and [0..cols-1]. The outermost row and column positions are walls. The playable area is [1..rows-2] by [1..cols-2].
A pipe may be placed only when all of the following are true:
1. The target coordinate is inside the playable area.
2. The target cell is a fillable cell.
3. The target cell does not already contain a pipe.

Cell Family
Cells are parsed from map characters into concrete cell types.
A fillable cell may contain a pipe or be empty. Empty fillable cells render as a dot.
A termination cell represents either the source or the sink. It stores the direction it points toward and whether it has been filled by water.
A wall cell represents the board boundary and renders using the wall character.

Termination Cell Rendering
A termination cell renders as an arrow based on its direction and filled state. Filled and unfilled arrows come from the shared rendering constants.
Both source and sink use their stored pointing direction directly. The direction is not reversed for either type.
Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

Pipe
A pipe has a shape and a filled state. Its shape determines the directions it connects to.
Supported pipe shapes include horizontal, vertical, the four corner shapes, and cross.
Pipe rendering depends on both shape and filled state. The rendered character should come from the shared rendering constants rather than hardcoded glyphs.
Short pipe codes include HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, and CR.

Water Flow
At the beginning of water flow, the source is marked as filled.
Water expands outward from the source by distance. Each update increases the reachable fill distance by one.
Water may move from one pipe to an adjacent pipe only when both sides connect to each other: the outgoing direction of one pipe must match the opposite direction of the neighboring pipe.
The filling process follows breadth-first expansion from the source.

Win and Loss Conditions
The player wins if there is a connected path from the source to the sink.
Path checking starts from the source by moving once in the source’s pointing direction. From each pipe, traversal continues through that pipe’s connection directions. The path does not require pipes to already be filled. Reaching the sink means the game is won.
The player loses when a water-flow update fills no new pipes. Loss is checked only after the initial delay has ended and water has actually started advancing.

Pipe Queue
The pipe queue stores upcoming pipes. The player always interacts with the pipe at the front.
When a pipe is consumed, the queue is automatically refilled up to a fixed maximum length using randomly generated pipe shapes.
Undo places the restored pipe back at the front of the queue.

Delay Bar
The delay bar starts with an initial value and decreases once per round.
While the value is still positive, water has not begun flowing. Once the countdown passes zero, the corresponding positive distance indicates how far water should expand from the source.

Cell Stack
The placement history records successfully placed cells in order.
Undo uses the most recent placement. The undo counter represents the number of successful undo actions, not the current size of the placement history.

External / Tool Classes
Pa19Helper is a handwritten helper and assembly class in the pa19 package. It is responsible for constructing core game objects from provided inputs, wiring together the map, pipe queue, delay bar, and placement history. It also centralizes basic parsing and direction utility behavior.
Deserializer is responsible for reading game input from a file and producing runtime game objects. It validates that the input file exists, reads the board size, delay value, map lines, and optional default pipes. If the input ends unexpectedly or an I/O error occurs, it returns no game object and logs the error.
When parsing map text, border cells are treated as sink-side termination candidates, while inner termination symbols are treated as source-side candidates.
Deserializer ignores blank lines and comment lines beginning with # when reading meaningful input.
PipePatterns is a final utility-style constant holder for all rendering characters. It contains the wall character and grouped constants for filled and unfilled arrows and pipe shapes. Rendering logic should rely on these constants instead of hardcoded characters.
StringUtils is a lightweight utility-style class for string helpers and should not be instantiated.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title pa19 Model

abstract class Cell {
    coord : Coordinate
}
class CellStack {
    count : int
    stack : List<FillableCell>
    getUndoCount() : int
    pop() : FillableCell
    push(cell : FillableCell) : void
}
class Coordinate {
    col : int
    row : int
    add(other : Coordinate) : Coordinate
    equals(obj : EJavaObject) : boolean
}
class DelayBar {
    currentValue : int
    initialValue : int
    countdown() : void
    distance() : int
}
class FillableCell {
    pipe : Pipe
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class Game {
    numOfSteps : int
    cellStack : CellStack
    delayBar : DelayBar
    map : Map
    pipeQueue : PipeQueue
    hasLost() : boolean
    hasWon() : boolean
    placePipe(row : int, col : char) : boolean
    skipPipe() : void
    undoStep() : boolean
    updateState() : void
}
class Map {
    cell : cellarray
    cols : int
    prevFilledDistance : EIntegerObject
    prevFilledTiles : int
    rows : int
    filledTiles : List<Coordinate>
    sinkCell : TerminationCell
    sourceCell : TerminationCell
    checkPath() : boolean
    fillBeginTile() : void
    fillTiles(distance : int) : void
    hasLost() : boolean
    tryPlacePipe(row : int, col : int, pipe : Pipe) : boolean
    tryPlacePipe1(coord : Coordinate, pipe : Pipe) : boolean
    undo(coord : Coordinate) : void
}
abstract class MapElement {
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class Pa19Helper {
    cellFromChar(c : char, coord : Coordinate, terminationType : TerminationType) : Cell
    createFillableCell(coord : Coordinate, pipes : Pipe) : FillableCell
    createGame(rows : int, cols : int, delay : int, cells : cellarray, pipes : List<Pipe>) : Game
    createMap(rows : int, cols : int, cells : cellarray) : Map
    createTerminationCell(coord : Coordinate, type : TerminationType, dir : Direction) : TerminationCell
    createWall(coord : Coordinate) : Wall
    gameFromString(rows : int, cols : int, delay : int, cellsRep : String, pipes : List<Pipe>) : Game
    getOffset(dir : Direction) : Coordinate
    getOpposite(dir : Direction) : Direction
    mapFromString(rows : int, cols : int, cellsRep : String) : Map
    pipeFromString(rep : String) : Pipe
}
class Pipe {
    filled : boolean
    shape : PipeShape
    getConnections() : List<Direction>
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class PipeQueue {
    maxGenLength : int
    pipeQueue : List<Pipe>
    consume() : void
    generateNewPipe() : Pipe
    peek() : Pipe
    undo(pipe : Pipe) : void
}
class TerminationCell {
    filled : boolean
    pointingTo : Direction
    type : TerminationType
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class Wall {
    toSingleChar() : char
}
enum Direction {
    UP
    DOWN
    LEFT
    RIGHT
}
enum PipeShape {
    HORIZONTAL
    VERTICAL
    TOP_LEFT
    TOP_RIGHT
    BOTTOM_LEFT
    BOTTOM_RIGHT
    CROSS
}
enum TerminationType {
    SOURCE
    SINK
}

Cell --|> MapElement
Cell *-- Coordinate : coord
CellStack *-- "*" FillableCell : stack
FillableCell --|> Cell
FillableCell *-- Pipe : pipe
Game *-- CellStack : cellStack
Game *-- DelayBar : delayBar
Game *-- Map : map
Game *-- PipeQueue : pipeQueue
Map *-- "*" Coordinate : filledTiles
Map --> TerminationCell : sinkCell
Map --> TerminationCell : sourceCell
Pipe --|> MapElement
PipeQueue *-- "*" Pipe : pipeQueue
TerminationCell --|> Cell
Wall --|> Cell
@enduml
```

```text
Undo restores the latest placed pipe to the front of the queue, clears that pipe from the board, and counts as one step. If there is no previous placement to undo, the operation fails without changing the board.

Map
The map stores cells in a two-dimensional grid with indices from [0..rows-1] and [0..cols-1]. The outermost row and column positions are walls. The playable area is [1..rows-2] by [1..cols-2].
A pipe may be placed only when all of the following are true:
1. The target coordinate is inside the playable area.
2. The target cell is a fillable cell.
3. The target cell does not already contain a pipe.

Cell Family
Cells are parsed from map characters into concrete cell types.
A fillable cell may contain a pipe or be empty. Empty fillable cells render as a dot.
A termination cell represents either the source or the sink. It stores the direction it points toward and whether it has been filled by water.
A wall cell represents the board boundary and renders using the wall character.

Termination Cell Rendering
A termination cell renders as an arrow based on its direction and filled state. Filled and unfilled arrows come from the shared rendering constants.
Both source and sink use their stored pointing direction directly. The direction is not reversed for either type.
Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

Pipe
A pipe has a shape and a filled state. Its shape determines the directions it connects to.
Supported pipe shapes include horizontal, vertical, the four corner shapes, and cross.
Pipe rendering depends on both shape and filled state. The rendered character should come from the shared rendering constants rather than hardcoded glyphs.
Short pipe codes include HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, and CR.

Water Flow
At the beginning of water flow, the source is marked as filled.
Water expands outward from the source by distance. Each update increases the reachable fill distance by one.
Water may move from one pipe to an adjacent pipe only when both sides connect to each other: the outgoing direction of one pipe must match the opposite direction of the neighboring pipe.
The filling process follows breadth-first expansion from the source.

Win and Loss Conditions
The player wins if there is a connected path from the source to the sink.
Path checking starts from the source by moving once in the source’s pointing direction. From each pipe, traversal continues through that pipe’s connection directions. The path does not require pipes to already be filled. Reaching the sink means the game is won.
The player loses when a water-flow update fills no new pipes. Loss is checked only after the initial delay has ended and water has actually started advancing.

Pipe Queue
The pipe queue stores upcoming pipes. The player always interacts with the pipe at the front.
When a pipe is consumed, the queue is automatically refilled up to a fixed maximum length using randomly generated pipe shapes.
Undo places the restored pipe back at the front of the queue.

Delay Bar
The delay bar starts with an initial value and decreases once per round.
While the value is still positive, water has not begun flowing. Once the countdown passes zero, the corresponding positive distance indicates how far water should expand from the source.

Cell Stack
The placement history records successfully placed cells in order.
Undo uses the most recent placement. The undo counter represents the number of successful undo actions, not the current size of the placement history.

External / Tool Classes
Pa19Helper is a handwritten helper and assembly class in the pa19 package. It is responsible for constructing core game objects from provided inputs, wiring together the map, pipe queue, delay bar, and placement history. It also centralizes basic parsing and direction utility behavior.
Deserializer is responsible for reading game input from a file and producing runtime game objects. It validates that the input file exists, reads the board size, delay value, map lines, and optional default pipes. If the input ends unexpectedly or an I/O error occurs, it returns no game object and logs the error.
When parsing map text, border cells are treated as sink-side termination candidates, while inner termination symbols are treated as source-side candidates.
Deserializer ignores blank lines and comment lines beginning with # when reading meaningful input.
PipePatterns is a final utility-style constant holder for all rendering characters. It contains the wall character and grouped constants for filled and unfilled arrows and pipe shapes. Rendering logic should rely on these constants instead of hardcoded characters.
StringUtils is a lightweight utility-style class for string helpers and should not be instantiated.
```

# Requirement
```text
Overall / Domain Description
This is a console pipe-connection game. The game board is a grid surrounded by outer walls. Inside the board there is one source, and on the border there is one sink. The player places pipes from a pipe queue. After an initial delay, water begins to flow through connected pipes.
The board uses zero-based internal array indices. However, index 0 and the last row/column are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts from internal index 1 and ends at rows - 2 / cols - 2.
Player-facing coordinates intentionally align with these playable internal indices:
row 1 refers to internal row 1, and column 'A' refers to internal column 1. No row-minus-one or column-minus-one conversion is applied, except that column letters are mapped so that 'A' becomes 1.
Directions include up, down, left, and right. Each direction has a corresponding opposite direction and a unit movement offset(class PA19Helper will help class Direction to provide these functions). Pipe shapes define which directions they connect to. Termination type distinguishes source and sink.

Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction
A coordinate stores the internal row and column used directly to access the cell grid. A cell stored at a given grid position should carry the same coordinate value.
Because the outer border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally begin at index 1. This allows user input to align directly with the playable internal grid.
Directions support movement calculations through their unit offsets and can be compared against their opposite direction when checking pipe connectivity.

Game
The game supports placing the current pipe, skipping the current pipe, undoing the latest placement, advancing the water state, checking whether the player has won, checking whether the player has lost, and tracking the number of player steps.
When the player places a pipe, the row value is used directly. The column letter is converted so that 'A' maps to column 1, 'B' maps to column 2, and so on. For example, row 1 and column 'A' target the internal cell at [1][1].
Skipping removes the current pipe from the front of the queue and counts as one step.
Undo restores the latest placed pipe to the front of the queue, clears that pipe from the board, and counts as one step. If there is no previous placement to undo, the operation fails without changing the board.

Map
The map stores cells in a two-dimensional grid with indices from [0..rows-1] and [0..cols-1]. The outermost row and column positions are walls. The playable area is [1..rows-2] by [1..cols-2].
A pipe may be placed only when all of the following are true:
1. The target coordinate is inside the playable area.
2. The target cell is a fillable cell.
3. The target cell does not already contain a pipe.

Cell Family
Cells are parsed from map characters into concrete cell types.
A fillable cell may contain a pipe or be empty. Empty fillable cells render as a dot.
A termination cell represents either the source or the sink. It stores the direction it points toward and whether it has been filled by water.
A wall cell represents the board boundary and renders using the wall character.

Termination Cell Rendering
A termination cell renders as an arrow based on its direction and filled state. Filled and unfilled arrows come from the shared rendering constants.
Both source and sink use their stored pointing direction directly. The direction is not reversed for either type.
Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

Pipe
A pipe has a shape and a filled state. Its shape determines the directions it connects to.
Supported pipe shapes include horizontal, vertical, the four corner shapes, and cross.
Pipe rendering depends on both shape and filled state. The rendered character should come from the shared rendering constants rather than hardcoded glyphs.
Short pipe codes include HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, and CR.

Water Flow
At the beginning of water flow, the source is marked as filled.
Water expands outward from the source by distance. Each update increases the reachable fill distance by one.
Water may move from one pipe to an adjacent pipe only when both sides connect to each other: the outgoing direction of one pipe must match the opposite direction of the neighboring pipe.
The filling process follows breadth-first expansion from the source.

Win and Loss Conditions
The player wins if there is a connected path from the source to the sink.
Path checking starts from the source by moving once in the source’s pointing direction. From each pipe, traversal continues through that pipe’s connection directions. The path does not require pipes to already be filled. Reaching the sink means the game is won.
The player loses when a water-flow update fills no new pipes. Loss is checked only after the initial delay has ended and water has actually started advancing.

Pipe Queue
The pipe queue stores upcoming pipes. The player always interacts with the pipe at the front.
When a pipe is consumed, the queue is automatically refilled up to a fixed maximum length using randomly generated pipe shapes.
Undo places the restored pipe back at the front of the queue.

Delay Bar
The delay bar starts with an initial value and decreases once per round.
While the value is still positive, water has not begun flowing. Once the countdown passes zero, the corresponding positive distance indicates how far water should expand from the source.

Cell Stack
The placement history records successfully placed cells in order.
Undo uses the most recent placement. The undo counter represents the number of successful undo actions, not the current size of the placement history.

External / Tool Classes
Pa19Helper is a handwritten helper and assembly class in the pa19 package. It is responsible for constructing core game objects from provided inputs, wiring together the map, pipe queue, delay bar, and placement history. It also centralizes basic parsing and direction utility behavior.
Deserializer is responsible for reading game input from a file and producing runtime game objects. It validates that the input file exists, reads the board size, delay value, map lines, and optional default pipes. If the input ends unexpectedly or an I/O error occurs, it returns no game object and logs the error.
When parsing map text, border cells are treated as sink-side termination candidates, while inner termination symbols are treated as source-side candidates.
Deserializer ignores blank lines and comment lines beginning with # when reading meaningful input.
PipePatterns is a final utility-style constant holder for all rendering characters. It contains the wall character and grouped constants for filled and unfilled arrows and pipe shapes. Rendering logic should rely on these constants instead of hardcoded characters.
StringUtils is a lightweight utility-style class for string helpers and should not be instantiated.
```

You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

# Requirement
```text
Overall / Domain Description
This is a console pipe-connection game. The game board is a grid surrounded by outer walls. Inside the board there is one source, and on the border there is one sink. The player places pipes from a pipe queue. After an initial delay, water begins to flow through connected pipes.
The board uses zero-based internal array indices. However, index 0 and the last row/column are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts from internal index 1 and ends at rows - 2 / cols - 2.
Player-facing coordinates intentionally align with these playable internal indices:
row 1 refers to internal row 1, and column 'A' refers to internal column 1. No row-minus-one or column-minus-one conversion is applied, except that column letters are mapped so that 'A' becomes 1.
Directions include up, down, left, and right. Each direction has a corresponding opposite direction and a unit movement offset(class PA19Helper will help class Direction to provide these functions). Pipe shapes define which directions they connect to. Termination type distinguishes source and sink.

Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction
A coordinate stores the internal row and column used directly to access the cell grid. A cell stored at a given grid position should carry the same coordinate value.
Because the outer border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally begin at index 1. This allows user input to align directly with the playable internal grid.
Directions support movement calculations through their unit offsets and can be compared against their opposite direction when checking pipe connectivity.

Game
The game supports placing the current pipe, skipping the current pipe, undoing the latest placement, advancing the water state, checking whether the player has won, checking whether the player has lost, and tracking the number of player steps.
When the player places a pipe, the row value is used directly. The column letter is converted so that 'A' maps to column 1, 'B' maps to column 2, and so on. For example, row 1 and column 'A' target the internal cell at [1][1].
Skipping removes the current pipe from the front of the queue and counts as one step.
Undo restores the latest placed pipe to the front of the queue, clears that pipe from the board, and counts as one step. If there is no previous placement to undo, the operation fails without changing the board.

Map
The map stores cells in a two-dimensional grid with indices from [0..rows-1] and [0..cols-1]. The outermost row and column positions are walls. The playable area is [1..rows-2] by [1..cols-2].
A pipe may be placed only when all of the following are true:
1. The target coordinate is inside the playable area.
2. The target cell is a fillable cell.
3. The target cell does not already contain a pipe.

Cell Family
Cells are parsed from map characters into concrete cell types.
A fillable cell may contain a pipe or be empty. Empty fillable cells render as a dot.
A termination cell represents either the source or the sink. It stores the direction it points toward and whether it has been filled by water.
A wall cell represents the board boundary and renders using the wall character.

Termination Cell Rendering
A termination cell renders as an arrow based on its direction and filled state. Filled and unfilled arrows come from the shared rendering constants.
Both source and sink use their stored pointing direction directly. The direction is not reversed for either type.
Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

Pipe
A pipe has a shape and a filled state. Its shape determines the directions it connects to.
Supported pipe shapes include horizontal, vertical, the four corner shapes, and cross.
Pipe rendering depends on both shape and filled state. The rendered character should come from the shared rendering constants rather than hardcoded glyphs.
Short pipe codes include HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, and CR.

Water Flow
At the beginning of water flow, the source is marked as filled.
Water expands outward from the source by distance. Each update increases the reachable fill distance by one.
Water may move from one pipe to an adjacent pipe only when both sides connect to each other: the outgoing direction of one pipe must match the opposite direction of the neighboring pipe.
The filling process follows breadth-first expansion from the source.

Win and Loss Conditions
The player wins if there is a connected path from the source to the sink.
Path checking starts from the source by moving once in the source’s pointing direction. From each pipe, traversal continues through that pipe’s connection directions. The path does not require pipes to already be filled. Reaching the sink means the game is won.
The player loses when a water-flow update fills no new pipes. Loss is checked only after the initial delay has ended and water has actually started advancing.

Pipe Queue
The pipe queue stores upcoming pipes. The player always interacts with the pipe at the front.
When a pipe is consumed, the queue is automatically refilled up to a fixed maximum length using randomly generated pipe shapes.
Undo places the restored pipe back at the front of the queue.

Delay Bar
The delay bar starts with an initial value and decreases once per round.
While the value is still positive, water has not begun flowing. Once the countdown passes zero, the corresponding positive distance indicates how far water should expand from the source.

Cell Stack
The placement history records successfully placed cells in order.
Undo uses the most recent placement. The undo counter represents the number of successful undo actions, not the current size of the placement history.

External / Tool Classes
Pa19Helper is a handwritten helper and assembly class in the pa19 package. It is responsible for constructing core game objects from provided inputs, wiring together the map, pipe queue, delay bar, and placement history. It also centralizes basic parsing and direction utility behavior.
Deserializer is responsible for reading game input from a file and producing runtime game objects. It validates that the input file exists, reads the board size, delay value, map lines, and optional default pipes. If the input ends unexpectedly or an I/O error occurs, it returns no game object and logs the error.
When parsing map text, border cells are treated as sink-side termination candidates, while inner termination symbols are treated as source-side candidates.
Deserializer ignores blank lines and comment lines beginning with # when reading meaningful input.
PipePatterns is a final utility-style constant holder for all rendering characters. It contains the wall character and grouped constants for filled and unfilled arrows and pipe shapes. Rendering logic should rely on these constants instead of hardcoded characters.
StringUtils is a lightweight utility-style class for string helpers and should not be instantiated.
```

30407 [main] INFO  du.ustb.sei.mde.mwe2.EcoreGenerator  - LLM Response:
{org.eclipse.emf.ecore.impl.EOperationImpl@182e7eda (name: undo) (ordered: true, unique: true, lowerBound: 0, upperBound: 1)=Summary: Restores a given pipe to the front of the pipe queue. This operation is used when the player undoes a placement, returning the pipe that was removed from the board back to the front of the queue so it can be reused.

Algorithm:
1. Insert the given pipe at index 0 of the pipeQueue list (the front of the queue).
2. No other modifications to the queue are needed; the queue may temporarily exceed its maxGenLength, but this is acceptable as the queue will be trimmed on subsequent consume operations.

Related Features: pipeQueue (the internal list of pipes), maxGenLength (though not directly modified here)

Related Classes: Pipe

Input: pipe : Pipe - The pipe object to be restored to the front of the queue. This is the pipe that was previously removed from the board during an undo operation.

Output: void - No return value.

Pre-condition: The pipe parameter must not be null. The pipeQueue list must be initialized and accessible.

Post-condition: The given pipe is added to the front (index 0) of the pipeQueue list. All previously queued pipes shift one position to the right. The queue size increases by 1. The pipe's state (shape and filled status) remains unchanged.}

# Operation Specifications
Game::hasWon
Summary: Checks whether the player has won the game. Win occurs when there is a connected path from source to sink.
Algorithm: Return map.checkPath().
Related Features: map field
Related Classes: Map
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if the player has won, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the win status from the map.

Game::hasLost
Summary: Checks whether the player has lost the game. Loss occurs when water has started flowing (delay passed) and a water update fills no new pipes.
Algorithm: Return map.hasLost().
Related Features: map field
Related Classes: Map
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if the player has lost, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the loss status from the map.

Game::placePipe
Summary: Attempts to place the current pipe from the queue onto the board at the specified coordinates. Converts column letter to internal column index (A=1, B=2, ...). If successful, consumes the pipe from queue and records the placement.
Algorithm: 1. Convert col char to int: colIndex = (int)(col - 'A' + 1). 2. Call map.tryPlacePipe(row, colIndex, pipeQueue.peek()). 3. If successful, call pipeQueue.consume(), push the placed cell onto cellStack, increment numOfSteps, return true. 4. Otherwise return false.
Related Features: map, pipeQueue, cellStack, numOfSteps fields
Related Classes: Map, PipeQueue, CellStack
Input: row : int - playable row index (1-based internal), col : char - column letter (A=1, B=2, ...)
Output: boolean - true if pipe was placed successfully, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: row and col must map to valid playable coordinates.
Post-condition: If successful, pipe is placed on map, pipe queue is consumed, cell is recorded in stack, steps incremented.

Game::updateState
Summary: Advances the game state by one round. Decrements the delay bar and if water should flow, updates water filling on the map.
Algorithm: 1. Call delayBar.countdown(). 2. If delayBar.distance() >= 0, call map.fillTiles(delayBar.distance()).
Related Features: delayBar, map fields
Related Classes: DelayBar, Map
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Delay bar is decremented. If water flow has started, tiles are filled to the current distance.

Game::skipPipe
Summary: Skips the current pipe at the front of the queue, removing it and counting as one step.
Algorithm: Call pipeQueue.consume(). Increment numOfSteps by 1.
Related Features: pipeQueue, numOfSteps fields
Related Classes: PipeQueue
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Current pipe is removed from queue, steps incremented.

Game::undoStep
Summary: Undoes the most recent pipe placement. Restores the pipe to the front of the queue, clears the cell on the board, and counts as one step. Fails if no placement history exists.
Algorithm: 1. Call cellStack.pop(). If null, return false. 2. Call map.undo(coord) on the popped cell's coordinate. 3. Call pipeQueue.undo(poppedCell.pipe). 4. Increment numOfSteps. 5. Return true.
Related Features: cellStack, map, pipeQueue, numOfSteps fields
Related Classes: CellStack, Map, PipeQueue
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if undo was successful, false if no placement to undo.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: If successful, the last placed pipe is removed from board and returned to queue front, steps incremented.

You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

# Requirement
```text
Overall / Domain Description
This is a console pipe-connection game. The game board is a grid surrounded by outer walls. Inside the board there is one source, and on the border there is one sink. The player places pipes from a pipe queue. After an initial delay, water begins to flow through connected pipes.
The board uses zero-based internal array indices. However, index 0 and the last row/column are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts from internal index 1 and ends at rows - 2 / cols - 2.
Player-facing coordinates intentionally align with these playable internal indices:
row 1 refers to internal row 1, and column 'A' refers to internal column 1. No row-minus-one or column-minus-one conversion is applied, except that column letters are mapped so that 'A' becomes 1.
Directions include up, down, left, and right. Each direction has a corresponding opposite direction and a unit movement offset(class PA19Helper will help class Direction to provide these functions). Pipe shapes define which directions they connect to. Termination type distinguishes source and sink.

Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction
A coordinate stores the internal row and column used directly to access the cell grid. A cell stored at a given grid position should carry the same coordinate value.
Because the outer border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally begin at index 1. This allows user input to align directly with the playable internal grid.
Directions support movement calculations through their unit offsets and can be compared against their opposite direction when checking pipe connectivity.

Game
The game supports placing the current pipe, skipping the current pipe, undoing the latest placement, advancing the water state, checking whether the player has won, checking whether the player has lost, and tracking the number of player steps.
When the player places a pipe, the row value is used directly. The column letter is converted so that 'A' maps to column 1, 'B' maps to column 2, and so on. For example, row 1 and column 'A' target the internal cell at [1][1].
Skipping removes the current pipe from the front of the queue and counts as one step.
Undo restores the latest placed pipe to the front of the queue, clears that pipe from the board, and counts as one step. If there is no previous placement to undo, the operation fails without changing the board.

Map
The map stores cells in a two-dimensional grid with indices from [0..rows-1] and [0..cols-1]. The outermost row and column positions are walls. The playable area is [1..rows-2] by [1..cols-2].
A pipe may be placed only when all of the following are true:
1. The target coordinate is inside the playable area.
2. The target cell is a fillable cell.
3. The target cell does not already contain a pipe.

Cell Family
Cells are parsed from map characters into concrete cell types.
A fillable cell may contain a pipe or be empty. Empty fillable cells render as a dot.
A termination cell represents either the source or the sink. It stores the direction it points toward and whether it has been filled by water.
A wall cell represents the board boundary and renders using the wall character.

Termination Cell Rendering
A termination cell renders as an arrow based on its direction and filled state. Filled and unfilled arrows come from the shared rendering constants.
Both source and sink use their stored pointing direction directly. The direction is not reversed for either type.
Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

Pipe
A pipe has a shape and a filled state. Its shape determines the directions it connects to.
Supported pipe shapes include horizontal, vertical, the four corner shapes, and cross.
Pipe rendering depends on both shape and filled state. The rendered character should come from the shared rendering constants rather than hardcoded glyphs.
Short pipe codes include HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, and CR.

Water Flow
At the beginning of water flow, the source is marked as filled.
Water expands outward from the source by distance. Each update increases the reachable fill distance by one.
Water may move from one pipe to an adjacent pipe only when both sides connect to each other: the outgoing direction of one pipe must match the opposite direction of the neighboring pipe.
The filling process follows breadth-first expansion from the source.

Win and Loss Conditions
The player wins if there is a connected path from the source to the sink.
Path checking starts from the source by moving once in the source’s pointing direction. From each pipe, traversal continues through that pipe’s connection directions. The path does not require pipes to already be filled. Reaching the sink means the game is won.
The player loses when a water-flow update fills no new pipes. Loss is checked only after the initial delay has ended and water has actually started advancing.

Pipe Queue
The pipe queue stores upcoming pipes. The player always interacts with the pipe at the front.
When a pipe is consumed, the queue is automatically refilled up to a fixed maximum length using randomly generated pipe shapes.
Undo places the restored pipe back at the front of the queue.

Delay Bar
The delay bar starts with an initial value and decreases once per round.
While the value is still positive, water has not begun flowing. Once the countdown passes zero, the corresponding positive distance indicates how far water should expand from the source.

Cell Stack
The placement history records successfully placed cells in order.
Undo uses the most recent placement. The undo counter represents the number of successful undo actions, not the current size of the placement history.

External / Tool Classes
Pa19Helper is a handwritten helper and assembly class in the pa19 package. It is responsible for constructing core game objects from provided inputs, wiring together the map, pipe queue, delay bar, and placement history. It also centralizes basic parsing and direction utility behavior.
Deserializer is responsible for reading game input from a file and producing runtime game objects. It validates that the input file exists, reads the board size, delay value, map lines, and optional default pipes. If the input ends unexpectedly or an I/O error occurs, it returns no game object and logs the error.
When parsing map text, border cells are treated as sink-side termination candidates, while inner termination symbols are treated as source-side candidates.
Deserializer ignores blank lines and comment lines beginning with # when reading meaningful input.
PipePatterns is a final utility-style constant holder for all rendering characters. It contains the wall character and grouped constants for filled and unfilled arrows and pipe shapes. Rendering logic should rely on these constants instead of hardcoded characters.
StringUtils is a lightweight utility-style class for string helpers and should not be instantiated.
```

```text
Deserializer ignores blank lines and comment lines beginning with # when reading meaningful input.
PipePatterns is a final utility-style constant holder for all rendering characters. It contains the wall character and grouped constants for filled and unfilled arrows and pipe shapes. Rendering logic should rely on these constants instead of hardcoded characters.
StringUtils is a lightweight utility-style class for string helpers and should not be instantiated.
```

# Operation Specifications
Game::hasWon
Summary: Checks whether the player has won the game. Win occurs when there is a connected path from source to sink.
Algorithm: Return map.checkPath().
Related Features: map field
Related Classes: Map
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if the player has won, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the win status from the map.

Game::hasLost
Summary: Checks whether the player has lost the game. Loss occurs when water has started flowing (delay passed) and a water update fills no new pipes.
Algorithm: Return map.hasLost().
Related Features: map field
Related Classes: Map
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if the player has lost, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the loss status from the map.

Game::placePipe
Summary: Attempts to place the current pipe from the queue onto the board at the specified coordinates. Converts column letter to internal column index (A=1, B=2, ...). If successful, consumes the pipe from queue and records the placement.
Algorithm: 1. Convert col char to int: colIndex = (int)(col - 'A' + 1). 2. Call map.tryPlacePipe(row, colIndex, pipeQueue.peek()). 3. If successful, call pipeQueue.consume(), push the placed cell onto cellStack, increment numOfSteps, return true. 4. Otherwise return false.
Related Features: map, pipeQueue, cellStack, numOfSteps fields
Related Classes: Map, PipeQueue, CellStack
Input: row : int - playable row index (1-based internal), col : char - column letter (A=1, B=2, ...)
Output: boolean - true if pipe was placed successfully, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: row and col must map to valid playable coordinates.
Post-condition: If successful, pipe is placed on map, pipe queue is consumed, cell is recorded in stack, steps incremented.

Game::updateState
Summary: Advances the game state by one round. Decrements the delay bar and if water should flow, updates water filling on the map.
Algorithm: 1. Call delayBar.countdown(). 2. If delayBar.distance() >= 0, call map.fillTiles(delayBar.distance()).
Related Features: delayBar, map fields
Related Classes: DelayBar, Map
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Delay bar is decremented. If water flow has started, tiles are filled to the current distance.

Game::skipPipe
Summary: Skips the current pipe at the front of the queue, removing it and counting as one step.
Algorithm: Call pipeQueue.consume(). Increment numOfSteps by 1.
Related Features: pipeQueue, numOfSteps fields
Related Classes: PipeQueue
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Current pipe is removed from queue, steps incremented.

Game::undoStep
Summary: Undoes the most recent pipe placement. Restores the pipe to the front of the queue, clears the cell on the board, and counts as one step. Fails if no placement history exists.
Algorithm: 1. Call cellStack.pop(). If null, return false. 2. Call map.undo(coord) on the popped cell's coordinate. 3. Call pipeQueue.undo(poppedCell.pipe). 4. Increment numOfSteps. 5. Return true.
Related Features: cellStack, map, pipeQueue, numOfSteps fields
Related Classes: CellStack, Map, PipeQueue
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if undo was successful, false if no placement to undo.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: If successful, the last placed pipe is removed from board and returned to queue front, steps incremented.

00:47:19.231 [main] DEBUG org.springframework.web.client.DefaultRestClient -- Writing [ChatCompletionRequest[messages=[ChatCompletionMessage[rawContent=# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Overall / Domain Description
This is a console pipe-connection game. The game board is a grid surrounded by outer walls. Inside the board there is one source, and on the border there is one sink. The player places pipes from a pipe queue. After an initial delay, water begins to flow through connected pipes.
The board uses zero-based internal array indices. However, index 0 and the last row/column are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts from internal index 1 and ends at rows - 2 / cols - 2.
Player-facing coordinates intentionally align with these playable internal indices:
row 1 refers to internal row 1, and column 'A' refers to internal column 1. No row-minus-one or column-minus-one conversion is applied, except that column letters are mapped so that 'A' becomes 1.
Directions include up, down, left, and right. Each direction has a corresponding opposite direction and a unit movement offset(class PA19Helper will help class Direction to provide these functions). Pipe shapes define which directions they connect to. Termination type distinguishes source and sink.

Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction
A coordinate stores the internal row and column used directly to access the cell grid. A cell stored at a given grid position should carry the same coordinate value.
Because the outer border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally begin at index 1. This allows user input to align directly with the playable internal grid.
Directions support movement calculations through their unit offsets and can be compared against their opposite direction when checking pipe connectivity.

Game
The game supports placing the current pipe, skipping the current pipe, undoing the latest placement, advancing the water state, checking whether the player has won, checking whether the player has lost, and tracking the number of player steps.
When the player places a pipe, the row value is used directly. The column letter is converted so that 'A' maps to column 1, 'B' maps to column 2, and so on. For example, row 1 and column 'A' target the internal cell at [1][1].
Skipping removes the current pipe from the front of the queue and counts as one step.
Undo restores the latest placed pipe to the front of the queue, clears that pipe from the board, and counts as one step. If there is no previous placement to undo, the operation fails without changing the board.

Map
The map stores cells in a two-dimensional grid with indices from [0..rows-1] and [0..cols-1]. The outermost row and column positions are walls. The playable area is [1..rows-2] by [1..cols-2].
A pipe may be placed only when all of the following are true:
1. The target coordinate is inside the playable area.
2. The target cell is a fillable cell.
3. The target cell does not already contain a pipe.

Cell Family
Cells are parsed from map characters into concrete cell types.
A fillable cell may contain a pipe or be empty. Empty fillable cells render as a dot.
A termination cell represents either the source or the sink. It stores the direction it points toward and whether it has been filled by water.
A wall cell represents the board boundary and renders using the wall character.

Termination Cell Rendering
A termination cell renders as an arrow based on its direction and filled state. Filled and unfilled arrows come from the shared rendering constants.
Both source and sink use their stored pointing direction directly. The direction is not reversed for either type.
Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

Pipe
A pipe has a shape and a filled state. Its shape determines the directions it connects to.
Supported pipe shapes include horizontal, vertical, the four corner shapes, and cross.
Pipe rendering depends on both shape and filled state. The rendered character should come from the shared rendering constants rather than hardcoded glyphs.
Short pipe codes include HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, and CR.

Water Flow
At the beginning of water flow, the source is marked as filled.
Water expands outward from the source by distance. Each update increases the reachable fill distance by one.
Water may move from one pipe to an adjacent pipe only when both sides connect to each other: the outgoing direction of one pipe must match the opposite direction of the neighboring pipe.
The filling process follows breadth-first expansion from the source.

Win and Loss Conditions
The player wins if there is a connected path from the source to the sink.
Path checking starts from the source by moving once in the source’s pointing direction. From each pipe, traversal continues through that pipe’s connection directions. The path does not require pipes to already be filled. Reaching the sink means the game is won.
The player loses when a water-flow update fills no new pipes. Loss is checked only after the initial delay has ended and water has actually started advancing.

Pipe Queue
The pipe queue stores upcoming pipes. The player always interacts with the pipe at the front.
When a pipe is consumed, the queue is automatically refilled up to a fixed maximum length using randomly generated pipe shapes.
Undo places the restored pipe back at the front of the queue.

Delay Bar
The delay bar starts with an initial value and decreases once per round.
While the value is still positive, water has not begun flowing. Once the countdown passes zero, the corresponding positive distance indicates how far water should expand from the source.

Cell Stack
The placement history records successfully placed cells in order.
Undo uses the most recent placement. The undo counter represents the number of successful undo actions, not the current size of the placement history.

External / Tool Classes
Pa19Helper is a handwritten helper and assembly class in the pa19 package. It is responsible for constructing core game objects from provided inputs, wiring together the map, pipe queue, delay bar, and placement history. It also centralizes basic parsing and direction utility behavior.
Deserializer is responsible for reading game input from a file and producing runtime game objects. It validates that the input file exists, reads the board size, delay value, map lines, and optional default pipes. If the input ends unexpectedly or an I/O error occurs, it returns no game object and logs the error.
When parsing map text, border cells are treated as sink-side termination candidates, while inner termination symbols are treated as source-side candidates.
Deserializer ignores blank lines and comment lines beginning with # when reading meaningful input.
PipePatterns is a final utility-style constant holder for all rendering characters. It contains the wall character and grouped constants for filled and unfilled arrows and pipe shapes. Rendering logic should rely on these constants instead of hardcoded characters.
StringUtils is a lightweight utility-style class for string helpers and should not be instantiated.
```

5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Overall / Domain Description
This is a console pipe-connection game. The game board is a grid surrounded by outer walls. Inside the board there is one source, and on the border there is one sink. The player places pipes from a pipe queue. After an initial delay, water begins to flow through connected pipes.
The board uses zero-based internal array indices. However, index 0 and the last row/column are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts from internal index 1 and ends at rows - 2 / cols - 2.
Player-facing coordinates intentionally align with these playable internal indices:
row 1 refers to internal row 1, and column 'A' refers to internal column 1. No row-minus-one or column-minus-one conversion is applied, except that column letters are mapped so that 'A' becomes 1.
Directions include up, down, left, and right. Each direction has a corresponding opposite direction and a unit movement offset(class PA19Helper will help class Direction to provide these functions). Pipe shapes define which directions they connect to. Termination type distinguishes source and sink.

Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction
A coordinate stores the internal row and column used directly to access the cell grid. A cell stored at a given grid position should carry the same coordinate value.
Because the outer border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally begin at index 1. This allows user input to align directly with the playable internal grid.
Directions support movement calculations through their unit offsets and can be compared against their opposite direction when checking pipe connectivity.

Game
The game supports placing the current pipe, skipping the current pipe, undoing the latest placement, advancing the water state, checking whether the player has won, checking whether the player has lost, and tracking the number of player steps.
When the player places a pipe, the row value is used directly. The column letter is converted so that 'A' maps to column 1, 'B' maps to column 2, and so on. For example, row 1 and column 'A' target the internal cell at [1][1].
Skipping removes the current pipe from the front of the queue and counts as one step.
Undo restores the latest placed pipe to the front of the queue, clears that pipe from the board, and counts as one step. If there is no previous placement to undo, the operation fails without changing the board.

Map
The map stores cells in a two-dimensional grid with indices from [0..rows-1] and [0..cols-1]. The outermost row and column positions are walls. The playable area is [1..rows-2] by [1..cols-2].
A pipe may be placed only when all of the following are true:
1. The target coordinate is inside the playable area.
2. The target cell is a fillable cell.
3. The target cell does not already contain a pipe.

Cell Family
Cells are parsed from map characters into concrete cell types.
A fillable cell may contain a pipe or be empty. Empty fillable cells render as a dot.
A termination cell represents either the source or the sink. It stores the direction it points toward and whether it has been filled by water.
A wall cell represents the board boundary and renders using the wall character.

Termination Cell Rendering
A termination cell renders as an arrow based on its direction and filled state. Filled and unfilled arrows come from the shared rendering constants.
Both source and sink use their stored pointing direction directly. The direction is not reversed for either type.
Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

Pipe
A pipe has a shape and a filled state. Its shape determines the directions it connects to.
Supported pipe shapes include horizontal, vertical, the four corner shapes, and cross.
Pipe rendering depends on both shape and filled state. The rendered character should come from the shared rendering constants rather than hardcoded glyphs.
Short pipe codes include HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, and CR.

Water Flow
At the beginning of water flow, the source is marked as filled.
Water expands outward from the source by distance. Each update increases the reachable fill distance by one.
Water may move from one pipe to an adjacent pipe only when both sides connect to each other: the outgoing direction of one pipe must match the opposite direction of the neighboring pipe.
The filling process follows breadth-first expansion from the source.

Win and Loss Conditions
The player wins if there is a connected path from the source to the sink.
Path checking starts from the source by moving once in the source’s pointing direction. From each pipe, traversal continues through that pipe’s connection directions. The path does not require pipes to already be filled. Reaching the sink means the game is won.
The player loses when a water-flow update fills no new pipes. Loss is checked only after the initial delay has ended and water has actually started advancing.

Pipe Queue
The pipe queue stores upcoming pipes. The player always interacts with the pipe at the front.
When a pipe is consumed, the queue is automatically refilled up to a fixed maximum length using randomly generated pipe shapes.
Undo places the restored pipe back at the front of the queue.

Delay Bar
The delay bar starts with an initial value and decreases once per round.
While the value is still positive, water has not begun flowing. Once the countdown passes zero, the corresponding positive distance indicates how far water should expand from the source.

Cell Stack
The placement history records successfully placed cells in order.
Undo uses the most recent placement. The undo counter represents the number of successful undo actions, not the current size of the placement history.

External / Tool Classes
Pa19Helper is a handwritten helper and assembly class in the pa19 package. It is responsible for constructing core game objects from provided inputs, wiring together the map, pipe queue, delay bar, and placement history. It also centralizes basic parsing and direction utility behavior.
Deserializer is responsible for reading game input from a file and producing runtime game objects. It validates that the input file exists, reads the board size, delay value, map lines, and optional default pipes. If the input ends unexpectedly or an I/O error occurs, it returns no game object and logs the error.
When parsing map text, border cells are treated as sink-side termination candidates, while inner termination symbols are treated as source-side candidates.
Deserializer ignores blank lines and comment lines beginning with # when reading meaningful input.
PipePatterns is a final utility-style constant holder for all rendering characters. It contains the wall character and grouped constants for filled and unfilled arrows and pipe shapes. Rendering logic should rely on these constants instead of hardcoded characters.
StringUtils is a lightweight utility-style class for string helpers and should not be instantiated.
```

# Class Diagram

# Operation Specifications
CellStack::getUndoCount
Summary: Returns the number of successful undo operations performed so far.
Algorithm: Return the value of count field.
Related Features: count field
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: int - the count of undo operations performed.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The count value is returned unchanged.

CellStack::pop
Summary: Removes and returns the most recently placed FillableCell from the stack.
Algorithm: If stack is not empty, remove and return the last element of stack. Otherwise return null.
Related Features: stack field
Related Classes: FillableCell
Input: None
Output: FillableCell - the most recently pushed cell, or null if stack is empty.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The last element is removed from stack if stack was not empty.

CellStack::push
Summary: Adds a FillableCell to the top of the placement history stack.
Algorithm: Add the given cell to the end of stack list.
Related Features: stack field
Related Classes: FillableCell
Input: cell : FillableCell - the cell to be recorded as placed
Output: void
Pre-condition: cell is not null
Post-condition: cell is added to the end of stack list.

```text
The board uses zero-based internal array indices. However, index 0 and the last row/column are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts from internal index 1 and ends at rows - 2 / cols - 2.
Player-facing coordinates intentionally align with these playable internal indices:
row 1 refers to internal row 1, and column 'A' refers to internal column 1. No row-minus-one or column-minus-one conversion is applied, except that column letters are mapped so that 'A' becomes 1.
Directions include up, down, left, and right. Each direction has a corresponding opposite direction and a unit movement offset(class PA19Helper will help class Direction to provide these functions). Pipe shapes define which directions they connect to. Termination type distinguishes source and sink.

Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction
A coordinate stores the internal row and column used directly to access the cell grid. A cell stored at a given grid position should carry the same coordinate value.
Because the outer border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally begin at index 1. This allows user input to align directly with the playable internal grid.
Directions support movement calculations through their unit offsets and can be compared against their opposite direction when checking pipe connectivity.

Game
The game supports placing the current pipe, skipping the current pipe, undoing the latest placement, advancing the water state, checking whether the player has won, checking whether the player has lost, and tracking the number of player steps.
When the player places a pipe, the row value is used directly. The column letter is converted so that 'A' maps to column 1, 'B' maps to column 2, and so on. For example, row 1 and column 'A' target the internal cell at [1][1].
Skipping removes the current pipe from the front of the queue and counts as one step.
Undo restores the latest placed pipe to the front of the queue, clears that pipe from the board, and counts as one step. If there is no previous placement to undo, the operation fails without changing the board.

Map
The map stores cells in a two-dimensional grid with indices from [0..rows-1] and [0..cols-1]. The outermost row and column positions are walls. The playable area is [1..rows-2] by [1..cols-2].
A pipe may be placed only when all of the following are true:
1. The target coordinate is inside the playable area.
2. The target cell is a fillable cell.
3. The target cell does not already contain a pipe.

Cell Family
Cells are parsed from map characters into concrete cell types.
A fillable cell may contain a pipe or be empty. Empty fillable cells render as a dot.
A termination cell represents either the source or the sink. It stores the direction it points toward and whether it has been filled by water.
A wall cell represents the board boundary and renders using the wall character.

Termination Cell Rendering
A termination cell renders as an arrow based on its direction and filled state. Filled and unfilled arrows come from the shared rendering constants.
Both source and sink use their stored pointing direction directly. The direction is not reversed for either type.
Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

Pipe
A pipe has a shape and a filled state. Its shape determines the directions it connects to.
Supported pipe shapes include horizontal, vertical, the four corner shapes, and cross.
Pipe rendering depends on both shape and filled state. The rendered character should come from the shared rendering constants rather than hardcoded glyphs.
Short pipe codes include HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, and CR.

Water Flow
At the beginning of water flow, the source is marked as filled.
Water expands outward from the source by distance. Each update increases the reachable fill distance by one.
Water may move from one pipe to an adjacent pipe only when both sides connect to each other: the outgoing direction of one pipe must match the opposite direction of the neighboring pipe.
The filling process follows breadth-first expansion from the source.

Win and Loss Conditions
The player wins if there is a connected path from the source to the sink.
Path checking starts from the source by moving once in the source’s pointing direction. From each pipe, traversal continues through that pipe’s connection directions. The path does not require pipes to already be filled. Reaching the sink means the game is won.
The player loses when a water-flow update fills no new pipes. Loss is checked only after the initial delay has ended and water has actually started advancing.

Pipe Queue
The pipe queue stores upcoming pipes. The player always interacts with the pipe at the front.
When a pipe is consumed, the queue is automatically refilled up to a fixed maximum length using randomly generated pipe shapes.
Undo places the restored pipe back at the front of the queue.

Delay Bar
The delay bar starts with an initial value and decreases once per round.
While the value is still positive, water has not begun flowing. Once the countdown passes zero, the corresponding positive distance indicates how far water should expand from the source.

Cell Stack
The placement history records successfully placed cells in order.
Undo uses the most recent placement. The undo counter represents the number of successful undo actions, not the current size of the placement history.

External / Tool Classes
Pa19Helper is a handwritten helper and assembly class in the pa19 package. It is responsible for constructing core game objects from provided inputs, wiring together the map, pipe queue, delay bar, and placement history. It also centralizes basic parsing and direction utility behavior.
Deserializer is responsible for reading game input from a file and producing runtime game objects. It validates that the input file exists, reads the board size, delay value, map lines, and optional default pipes. If the input ends unexpectedly or an I/O error occurs, it returns no game object and logs the error.
When parsing map text, border cells are treated as sink-side termination candidates, while inner termination symbols are treated as source-side candidates.
Deserializer ignores blank lines and comment lines beginning with # when reading meaningful input.
PipePatterns is a final utility-style constant holder for all rendering characters. It contains the wall character and grouped constants for filled and unfilled arrows and pipe shapes. Rendering logic should rely on these constants instead of hardcoded characters.
StringUtils is a lightweight utility-style class for string helpers and should not be instantiated.
```

```plantuml
title pa19 Model

abstract class Cell {
    coord : Coordinate
}
class CellStack {

    stack : List<FillableCell>

    row : int

    equals(obj : EJavaObject) : boolean
}

    currentValue : int
    initialValue : int
    countdown() : void
    distance() : int
}

    pipe : Pipe
    toSingleChar() : char
}

    numOfSteps : int
    cellStack : CellStack
    delayBar : DelayBar
    map : Map
    pipeQueue : PipeQueue
    hasLost() : boolean
    hasWon() : boolean

    skipPipe() : void

    updateState() : void
}

    cell : cellarray
    cols : int

    sourceCell : TerminationCell

    toSingleChar() : char
}

    createMap(rows : int, cols : int, cells : cellarray) : Map
    createTerminationCell(coord : Coordinate, type : TerminationType, dir : Direction) : TerminationCell
    createWall(coord : Coordinate) : Wall
    gameFromString(rows : int, cols : int, delay : int, cellsRep : String, pipes : List<Pipe>) : Game
    getOffset(dir : Direction) : Coordinate
    getOpposite(dir : Direction) : Direction
    mapFromString(rows : int, cols : int, cellsRep : String) : Map
    pipeFromString(rep : String) : Pipe
}
class Pipe {
    filled : boolean
    shape : PipeShape
    getConnections() : List<Direction>
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class PipeQueue {
    maxGenLength : int
    pipeQueue : List<Pipe>
    consume() : void
    generateNewPipe() : Pipe
    peek() : Pipe
    undo(pipe : Pipe) : void
}
class TerminationCell {
    filled : boolean
    pointingTo : Direction
    type : TerminationType
    toSingleChar() : char
}

    toSingleChar() : char
}

    UP
    DOWN
    LEFT
    RIGHT
}

    HORIZONTAL
    VERTICAL
    TOP_LEFT
    TOP_RIGHT
    BOTTOM_LEFT
    BOTTOM_RIGHT
    CROSS
}

    SOURCE
    SINK
}
```

# Operation Specifications
Pipe::getConnections
Summary: Returns the list of directions that this pipe connects to, based on its shape.
Algorithm: Switch on shape: HORIZONTAL -> [LEFT, RIGHT]; VERTICAL -> [UP, DOWN]; TOP_LEFT -> [UP, LEFT]; TOP_RIGHT -> [UP, RIGHT]; BOTTOM_LEFT -> [DOWN, LEFT]; BOTTOM_RIGHT -> [DOWN, RIGHT]; CROSS -> [UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT].
Related Features: shape field
Related Classes: Direction, PipeShape
Input: None
Output: List<Direction> - list of connection directions for this pipe shape
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the appropriate connection directions for the pipe's shape.

# Requirement
```text
Overall / Domain Description
This is a console pipe-connection game. The game board is a grid surrounded by outer walls. Inside the board there is one source, and on the border there is one sink. The player places pipes from a pipe queue. After an initial delay, water begins to flow through connected pipes.
The board uses zero-based internal array indices. However, index 0 and the last row/column are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts from internal index 1 and ends at rows - 2 / cols - 2.

Pipe shapes define which directions they connect to. Termination type distinguishes source and sink.

Water expands outward from the source by distance. Each update increases the reachable fill distance by one.
Water may move from one pipe to an adjacent pipe only when both sides connect to each other: the outgoing direction of one pipe must match the opposite direction of the neighboring pipe.
The filling process follows breadth-first expansion from the source.

Win and Loss Conditions
The player wins if there is a connected path from the source to the sink.
Path checking starts from the source by moving once in the source’s pointing direction. From each pipe, traversal continues through that pipe’s connection directions. The path does not require pipes to already be filled. Reaching the sink means the game is won.
The player loses when a water-flow update fills no new pipes. Loss is checked only after the initial delay has ended and water has actually started advancing.

Pipe Queue
The pipe queue stores upcoming pipes. The player always interacts with the pipe at the front.
When a pipe is consumed, the queue is automatically refilled up to a fixed maximum length using randomly generated pipe shapes.
Undo places the restored pipe back at the front of the queue.

Delay Bar
The delay bar starts with an initial value and decreases once per round.
While the value is still positive, water has not begun flowing. Once the countdown passes zero, the corresponding positive distance indicates how far water should expand from the source.

Cell Stack
The placement history records successfully placed cells in order.
Undo uses the most recent placement. The undo counter represents the number of successful undo actions, not the current size of the placement history.

External / Tool Classes
Pa19Helper is a handwritten helper and assembly class in the pa19 package. It is responsible for constructing core game objects from provided inputs, wiring together the map, pipe queue, delay bar, and placement history. It also centralizes basic parsing and direction utility behavior.
Deserializer is responsible for reading game input from a file and producing runtime game objects. It validates that the input file exists, reads the board size, delay value, map lines, and optional default pipes. If the input ends unexpectedly or an I/O error occurs, it returns no game object and logs the error.
When parsing map text, border cells are treated as sink-side termination candidates, while inner termination symbols are treated as source-side candidates.
Deserializer ignores blank lines and comment lines beginning with # when reading meaningful input.
PipePatterns is a final utility-style constant holder for all rendering characters. It contains the wall character and grouped constants for filled and unfilled arrows and pipe shapes. Rendering logic should rely on these constants instead of hardcoded characters.
StringUtils is a lightweight utility-style class for string helpers and should not be instantiated.
```

# Operation Specifications
Map::fillTiles
Summary: Performs breadth-first water expansion from the source up to the given distance. Marks source as filled initially. Each step expands to adjacent connected pipes.
Algorithm: 1. If distance == 0, call fillBeginTile() to mark source as filled. 2. For each distance step from 1 to distance, iterate over currently filled tiles and attempt to expand to adjacent cells. For each filled tile, check its four neighbors. If neighbor has a pipe and both pipes connect (pipe's outgoing direction matches neighbor's opposite direction), and neighbor is not already filled, mark it as filled and add to filledTiles. 3. Update prevFilledTiles count.
Related Features: filledTiles, sourceCell, sinkCell, prevFilledTiles fields
Related Classes: Coordinate, Direction, Pipe, TerminationCell
Input: distance : int - the current expansion distance from source
Output: void
Pre-condition: distance >= 0
Post-condition: Water has expanded to fill all reachable pipes within the given distance from source.

Map::tryPlacePipe
Summary: Attempts to place a pipe at the specified cell coordinate. Validates that the coordinate is within playable area, the cell is fillable, and the cell is empty.
Algorithm: 1. Check if row is between 1 and rows-2, col between 1 and cols-2. If not, return false. 2. Get cell at [row][col]. 3. If cell is FillableCell and cell.pipe is null, set cell.pipe = pipe, return true. 4. Otherwise return false.
Related Features: cell array, rows, cols fields
Related Classes: FillableCell, Pipe, Coordinate
Input: row : int, col : int - internal grid coordinates
Output: boolean - true if pipe was placed, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: If successful, the pipe is placed in the target cell.

Map::undo
Summary: Removes the pipe from a cell at the given coordinate, clearing it for future placement.
Algorithm: Get cell at coord. If cell is FillableCell, set cell.pipe = null. Remove coord from filledTiles if present.
Related Features: cell array, filledTiles fields
Related Classes: FillableCell, Coordinate
Input: coord : Coordinate - the coordinate of the cell to undo
Output: void
Pre-condition: coord is within bounds
Post-condition: The pipe at the specified coordinate is removed, and the cell is cleared.

Map::hasLost
Summary: Checks if the player has lost. Loss occurs when water has started flowing and the most recent fillTiles call did not fill any new pipes.
Algorithm: Return (prevFilledDistance >= 0) && (prevFilledTiles == filledTiles.size()).
Related Features: prevFilledDistance, prevFilledTiles, filledTiles fields
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if lost, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns loss status based on whether new tiles were filled.
